## 4. T24 Programming Training Series ｜ Key concepts of Subroutines and Functions
Let's get started. Here is a sample of a subruine. As you can see, a subruine starts with the keyword subruine followed by the name of subruine and in parentheses we put both inward parameters and outward parameters. Our sub routine is called calculate DTI and it has three parameters. Two of which are inward parameters, gross income and total deaths and one outward parameters. As you can see, we determine debt to income ratio as total debts over gross income. So when invoking our sub routine it is going to calculate this DTI and return it here. So we don't specify this value here after return. We simply determine the value and write the keyword return and end. Now let's see a sample of a function. A function starts with a keyword function followed by the name of a function and in parentheses we put only inward parameters. We don't specify outward parameters. So our function here is called DTI. It has two parameters gross income and total debts. Then we can determine the DTI the same way we did in sub routine and return the value. So our value to be returned is written after the keyword return. So here we are returning DTI as total debts over income. the same way we did here in sub routine. But the difference as you can see on subruine the return value is not written after return. Why? In a function the return value needs to come after the keyword return. And then you also have end the same as sub routine. Now let's see how to use subruine and how to use function. First of all, both sub routines and functions cannot be invoked from gesture. So we need to invoke a subruine or a function from another subruine or from programs. So to invoke a subruine we write keyword call followed by the name of subruine and in parentheses we put all the parameters in the same way as they have been defined when creating that subruine. So you use the signature of a subruine the way it is this part. Now how do we call a function? Before invoking a function, we need to declare that function. You declare a function using the keyword define followed by the name of a function and parenthesis. To call a function, you simply write the name of a function and in parenthesis you put the inword parameters. You can assign the return value to a variable. This val is a local variable. Now let's move to a practical session. Let's see how to use function and sub routine. We are going to use the same programs. We've just seen the same subruine and the same function. So as you can see here, I have this sub routine already created and I also have the function. So I've already created these sub routines. I compiled and cataloged. You need to do the same before you invoke them. Now let's write a simple program in which we are going to invoke both our sub routine and function. Let me create a program. As you have seen in our presentation to invoke a subruine we need to write the name of our sub routine and all the parameters. So we type call and the name of our sub routine with all parameters inward and outwards. Let's quickly me verify. Okay, they're the same. So when the program is going to call this sub routine, it's going to have DTI as a return value. So we can use now this value. For now, we are just going to print this value. Let's say the message DTI from sub routine is okay and then you put DTI okay now let's use function to use a function as you have seen we need to declare the function And to declare the function we use the keyword define. So let's declare our function here. The top we do define and the name of our function is dti. Okay here it is. And to invoke our function, we just type the name of our function and in parenthesis we put the parameters it takes. So our function takes these two parameters. ## Temenos T24 Arrangement Architecture Training ｜ Fundamentals of AA Module
[Music] hello welcome to mathis digital my name is aaron and today we are going to continue our t24 business training we are going to see temenos t24 arrangement architecture by the end of this training session you're going to have a good understanding on the fundamentals of t24 arrangement architecture the product life cycle in a module and how to create new products or amend the existing one using a product builder so this is kind of introduction on am a module is really huge module and we cannot cover this in one video but the knowledge you are going to accumulate through this training session will be sufficient for you to build upon to learn more about aaa and how to use a module now there's some terminologies which are used in a module and it is very important that you get understanding on those terminologies before you proceed with a module first of all we are going to see what are product lines and property classes you can think of property classes as components on high level which are used to build a product line those high level components have attributes and actions these are reusable components meaning we can have components of the same building blocks across different product lines for instance if we take a product line such as accounts and deposits you will find that we have property class called account which is used across all these product lines same as interest because accounts and deposits and lending products they all have interests okay if you don't understand now don't worry as you progress you're going to understand so a product line is made of different property classes so meaning you take different components together and they make a product line product lines and property classes are defined for us by terminals and they're maintained by terminals financial institutions cannot modify this so probably the only thing they can modify is description but they cannot define new product lines okay this sole responsibility of terminals now on the middle layer or on the second layer we have product groups and properties now as you can guess product groups are subsets of product lines and properties are named types of property classes meaning we create product groups from product lines which have been defined by terminals so both property groups and the properties can be defined and maintained by financial institutions a product group is made of different properties which you put together to create a product a group the bank do not sell product groups okay because the product groups are just there to group different products with similar properties now after defining product groups the bank will create products products are also made of properties but at this level we need to define product conditions you can think of product conditions as default values for those properties which make a product so when defining a product you specify the properties and their default values and for each property which is negotiable you need to define the restrictions for instance if you say an interest rate is five percent but negotiable then you need to define what are what is the maximum and what is the minimum or at least by how much you can negotiate up and down so these are restrictions you can also say this is non-negotiable meaning when a user is going to create the arrangement for that product those conditions won't be won't be negotiable they want to change they want to have access to change those conditions and finally we have arrangements and arrangement conditions so if a customer comes and say i want current account a normal current account let's say let's call it a normal current account okay now there are conditions that govern that normal account probabilities arrangement as that control agreement between a financial institution and a client with all the conditions defined now before we proceed with the practical session let's also try to understand activity classes activity classes define a system behavior when a certain activity is triggered or run so the way they find the id includes a product line then activity or the process which is going okay help you understand now let's discuss briefly on the lifecycle of products in aaa module there are three stages so each product we go into these three stages the first stage is design stage so in the design stage we define all the properties and the product conditions for certain products so we're creating this product designing that product after designing that product meaning after defining all those properties and the conditions attached to those properties we need to proof our design so we need we need to validate our design the system will check if the conditions we have defined are correct in this proof stage once they are validated there are no errors then we can publish that product when we publish our product goes to the product catalog and now user have access to that
